# Human actions

Work only a person can do (master prompt sections 3, 19 and 20). The build never waits for these;
they gate a real tenant going live, which is Brian's decision. Each item says what the build has
already produced for it. Update the status column as items are done.

**Owner instruction, 31/10/2026 ([ADR-0098](../decisions/0098-production-on-google-cloud.md)):** every
open question below, whoever owns it, takes its recommended answer: the default in force where there is
one, otherwise the recommendation in [recommended-answers.md](recommended-answers.md), so that development
never waits. All of them are reviewed at the testing phase after development, before
any real tenant goes live. Sign-offs, accounts, appointments and contracts still need the people named.

Owners: **Brian** (Thapsus Cargo Ltd), **CSO** (Clinical Safety Officer, to be appointed), **DPO**
(data protection officer or named lead), **Tenant** (each care provider at onboarding).

| H-397 | Accept or reject the dependency exception for `decode-uri-component` (GHSA-qr28-fpjm-wq7f, CVE-2026-118664) added on 31/10/2026. It does not meet the usual rule: a fixed release (0.5.1) exists but is ESM-only and cannot be loaded by `query-string` 7, which `expo-router` uses, and the slow decoding was thought reachable in the mobile app through a crafted link (the app would freeze on that device; no data exposed and no server affected; corrected on 06/11/2026: `expo-router` 57 does not use this decoder when it parses a link, see the status). If rejected, the alternative is moving the app to an Expo SDK whose router no longer uses `query-string` 7 | Brian | [vulnerability-exceptions.md](../compliance/vulnerability-exceptions.md) row, `.trivyignore` entry expiring 30/11/2026, `pnpm.auditConfig.ignoreGhsas` | Closing the advisory before the exception expires | decided 31/10/2026: default accepted (owner); 06/11/2026: the patch replaced the exception: `patches/decode-uri-component@0.2.2.patch` swaps in a CommonJS port of 0.5.1's linear-time decoder, which fixes GHSA-qr28-fpjm-wq7f and GHSA-vcc3-ghjq-m6fr, with tests (`apps/mobile/src/platform/decode-uri-component.test.ts`); the ids stay in the ignore lists only because `pnpm audit` and Trivy match by version number, so no risk is accepted any more (Claude Code); 06/11/2026 correction: the patched copy is dormant on today's link path. `expo-router` 57 parses a link's query with `URLSearchParams` in its own `getStateFromPath`, screens read their parameters with `useLocalSearchParams` (the built-in decoder in a `try`/`catch`), and `query-string` is loaded only to build paths and by React Navigation's own parser, which `expo-router` replaces. The patch is defence in depth; a test sends a crafted 200 KB link through `expo-router`'s own `getStateFromPath` and shows it never reaches this decoder (Claude Code) |

| H-126 | Rate HZ-040 (held entries deleted to get past the lockout) after rounds 5 and 6. Nothing is deleted automatically; a hold escalates to a manager at `mobile.held_entries_max_days` on the device. Once the device's report reaches the server, it raises a task due at the same time for a Registered Manager: at each location of the device's sync scope where the hold's owner or the reporter works or last worked (else the reporter's live locations, then their most recent one, including one just removed, as an agency worker's is at the end of the shift), or, when no location is known, one for each Registered Manager in the organisation; overdue, it escalates only to holders of `safety.incident.manage` there. The hold's owner, whoever raised the task, or a holder of `safety.incident.manage` at one of its locations closes it (H-142). A task closed less than a day ago is reopened, its escalation reset at most once a day, so a new hold always signals; past a reporter's daily cap, holds join one task for that reporter rather than raising none (H-149). A named owner counts only when they have used the device (a sync token issued to it, or a report sent from it, kept in `device_binding`); otherwise the claim is recorded and the task follows the reporter (security review round 7, M1). A hold whose session ended with a 401, `not_a_member` or `mfa_enrolment_required` is reported only when someone signs in on that device again: the owner's own sign-in sends the report, as does another member's in the organisation, and in that forwarded case the task goes where the owner (who has used the device) or that member works and the owner may close it; until then the server knows only the last report (H-141). Deleting needs the owner's or a manager's sign-in and the entry ids (without ids only when no key exists or the key is not a valid one, recorded with that reason: H-144), and is recorded at the API in stages: requested before anything is deleted, with the entries marked never to be uploaded (a keystore write; if it fails, nothing is deleted); "deleted" once the entries can no longer be read, any file or key left behind recorded as a security event; "failed", superseding the request, only while the file, its key and the rows all remain, after which the entries stay held and never upload (H-143). Entries uploaded after their deletion was recorded as done, or listed by a deletion after they had reached the server, are flagged to the Registered Manager at their location on one task per deletion record and location, due at once when any is an observation and otherwise within the day (H-147, H-148). The proposed residual stays Major / Low / 3 with the round 6 and 7 fixes, and is not to be rated lower until the location routing (clinical review round 5, C-H1) and the meaning of "failed" (C-M1) are confirmed on a device; the manager hand-over upload, device revocation and the indexed deletion lists have landed (Device integrity; H-156, H-158). Accept or reject the level-3 residual risk, and the causes assessed though now removed or controlled (automatic expiry, refused content ageing out, a deletion recorded at the server that then failed on the device) | CSO | [Hazard log](../clinical-safety/hazard-log.md) HZ-040; tests in `apps/mobile/src/app-state/`, `apps/mobile/src/sync/`, `apps/api/test/api.test.ts`, `apps/api/test/care.test.ts` and `apps/api/test/tasks.test.ts` | Care mode on a live tenant | provisional: owner-accepted recommendation, pending CSO review at the testing phase (ADR-0132) |

| H-141 | Accept the reworded residual of the held-device report (clinical reviews round 5, C-M4 and round 6, M3): a hold whose session ended with a 401, `not_a_member` or `mfa_enrolment_required` is reported only when someone signs in on that device again. The owner's own sign-in sends the report, as does another member's in the organisation; in that forwarded case the task goes where the owner (who has used the device) or that member works, and the owner may close it (H-142). Until then the server knows only the device's last report, so a device lost or left in a drawer with entries held may never raise a task. Decide whether a server-side stale-device check (a task for the Registered Manager when a device's last report or sync goes stale) is a go-live blocker. The table binding devices to their users now exists (`device_binding`, with first and last seen and the sessions bound to each; Device integrity part 1), so the check can be built once this is decided; a revoked device's hold is now due at once (#25 review round 2, M4). A revoked device's sessions are no longer ended, so the device is told `device_revoked` rather than a 401 and reports its hold at once, and the device list and a revocation give when each device last reported (`lastReportedAt`), the figure a stale-device check would use (#25 review round 3, M2) | CSO | [Hazard log](../clinical-safety/hazard-log.md) HZ-006 and HZ-040; `apps/api/src/services/device-holds.ts` | Care mode on a live tenant | provisional: owner-accepted recommendation, pending CSO review at the testing phase (ADR-0132) |
| H-142 | Decide who receives and may close the Care mode sync tasks (clinical reviews rounds 6 and 7). Today a device-hold task goes to a Registered Manager at each location of the device's sync scope where the hold's owner or the reporter works or last worked (else the reporter's live locations, then their most recent one), or to each Registered Manager when no location is known; overdue it escalates only to holders of `safety.incident.manage` at its location (not team leaders, and not holders elsewhere). Every copy closes together, on a report with nothing waiting or a recorded deletion of the device's held entries, from the hold's owner, from whoever raised it, or from a holder of `safety.incident.manage` at one of its locations; anyone else's close is refused and audited. With no one at the location who can act (a vacant post), an overdue task is assigned to the longest-serving live Registered Manager anywhere in the organisation, or when there is none to the longest-serving holder of `safety.incident.manage` anywhere; that person alone is told and, as its assignee, can see and complete it although their own role covers another location; with no one at all it is never marked escalated, the gap is audited once a day and the task waits a day before it is tried again. Decide whether that is the right person, or name another (for example the Nominated Individual). A named owner counts only when they have used the device. A revoked device's hold falls due at once and urgent, and the manager who revoked it is told (#25 review round 2, M4); the refused-entry review escalates the same way, with the same fallback (M3; H-157). A flagged-entry task (entries uploaded after their deletion was recorded, or listed by a deletion after they had reached the server) is one per deletion record and location (a unique index on open tasks now enforces one per source, Device integrity part 4), goes to the Registered Manager role at the entries' location and escalates the same way. Decide: (1) the roles, or name others (a deputy, the nurse in charge); (2) whether the hold's owner may close the task: today they may, so the owner's own drain or deletion closes a task another member's sign-in raised for them; (3) the caps: no cap stops a task any more, it merges them (clinical review round 7, M2; accepted or not under H-149): a reporter raises tasks for at most 3 devices a day, and further holds join one capped-holds task for that reporter, due at the earliest of them; an owner's deletions have at most 5 flagged-entry tasks open at a location, and further entries join the oldest of them; each cap is also audited and the location's Registered Managers get one notice a day about that member of staff | CSO | [Hazard log](../clinical-safety/hazard-log.md) HZ-008 and HZ-040; `apps/api/src/services/device-holds.ts`, `apps/api/src/services/tasks.ts` | Care mode on a live tenant | provisional: owner-accepted recommendation, pending CSO review at the testing phase (ADR-0132) |
| H-143 | Decide what "deletion failed" means for the record (clinical review round 5, C-M1). Today "failed" is recorded only while the entries can still be read on the device (the database file, its key and the rows inside all remain): the entries then stay held, are never uploaded, and move to "needs attention" for their owner. When they cannot be read but part of the database is left (a file without its key, or a key without its file), "deleted" is recorded and the leftover is recorded as a separate security event. Confirm that a record of a deleted entry and a security event for the leftover is the right reading for the care record and for an inspector | CSO | [Hazard log](../clinical-safety/hazard-log.md) HZ-040; `apps/mobile/src/app-state/held-work.ts` (`deletionOutcome`) | Care mode on a live tenant | provisional: owner-accepted recommendation, pending CSO review at the testing phase (ADR-0132) |
| H-144 | Decide whether a deletion of unsent entries without their ids is ever acceptable (clinical review round 5, C-M2). Today a deletion must say which entries it removes; a cancelled face or fingerprint prompt, or entries that cannot be read, refuse it and ask to try again. It goes ahead without ids only when nothing on the device could ever be read (no key, or a key that is not a valid one), and the reason is recorded at the server. The alternative is to refuse even then, leaving an unreadable file on the device until it is reset | CSO | [Hazard log](../clinical-safety/hazard-log.md) HZ-040; `apps/mobile/src/app-state/held-work.ts` (`readHeldIds`) | Care mode on a live tenant | provisional: owner-accepted recommendation, pending CSO review at the testing phase (ADR-0132) |
| H-145 | Decide whether the care record should leave a phone at once when the access Care mode needs is withdrawn (clinical review round 5, C-L9). Today any refusal from the API on the sync token (a role removed, a permission edited, a feature switched off) ends Care mode on the device straight away: everything synced except the unsent work (allergies, resuscitation decisions, IDDSI levels, care plans) leaves the device, so a role edit made by mistake mid-shift leaves that care worker without essential information until it is put right. | H-146 | Confirm that a deletion's reason in the person's own words never reaches the server (clinical review round 5, C-L10). Thapsus Care records only the coded reason, including "Other"; deleting held entries or signing out offers no "Other" and no words, and words given when removing one refused entry stay encrypted on the device until the server has the deletion, then go. An inspector reading an "Other" deletion sees no explanation. | H-148 | Decide whether 24 hours is acceptable for flagged-entry tasks that hold no observation (clinical review round 7, M3): notes, body-map marks, activities and visits uploaded after their deletion was recorded, or listed by a deletion after they had reached the server. A task holding any observation (a chart) is due at once; one holding only these kinds is due 24 hours after it is raised, so a possible duplicate note or body-map mark may stand unreviewed for up to a day. A flagged care note, activity record or assessment can also complete a scheduled task it answers (`apps/api/src/routes/care.ts` and `care-records.ts`), so a round may show as done for up to 24 hours on the strength of an entry that may be a duplicate. Accept 24 hours, set a shorter window, or make every flagged task due at once | CSO | [Hazard log](../clinical-safety/hazard-log.md) HZ-008 and HZ-040; `flaggedDue` in `apps/api/src/services/device-holds.ts` | Care mode on a live tenant | recommended answer applied 31/10/2026 (review at testing) |
| H-149 | Accept that the Care mode sync caps merge tasks rather than stop them (clinical review round 7, M2). Past a reporter's 3 devices a day, each further hold joins that reporter's one capped-holds task, due when the earliest of those holds escalates and escalating like a device's task, which a manager completes once the devices are dealt with; past an owner's 5 open flagged-entry tasks at a location, further entries join the oldest of them, which then counts and lists them and falls due sooner when they call for it. Every cap is audited, and the location's Registered Managers also get one notice a day per member of staff. The trade-off: one task can stand for several devices or deletions, so the manager reads its audit trail (or the devices) to see them all | CSO | [Hazard log](../clinical-safety/hazard-log.md) HZ-008 and HZ-040; `mergeCappedHold` and `raiseFlaggedTaskAt` in `apps/api/src/services/device-holds.ts` | Care mode on a live tenant | provisional: owner-accepted recommendation, pending CSO review at the testing phase (ADR-0132) |
| H-157 | Confirm the review of refused entries' copies ([care.md](../specs/care.md) section 6, Device integrity part 5; HZ-006, HZ-008; #25 review round 2, M2 and M3). Defaults in force: the device sends the request it made for every care entry the API refuses (checked against that entry kind's contract), and the server keeps a copy (`refused_entry`) only about someone its author cared for within the offline window (otherwise refused like a copy about someone unknown); the copy joins the person's one open review task for the nurse in charge (`care.refused_entry_review`, holders of `care.plan.write` at the person's location), due at once and urgent when any copy is an observation, when the nurse in charge (the clinical leads and team leaders whose roles reach the home, at every home; no rota is checked) whose people.person.read and care.note.read reach the person, and the holders of care.plan.write who can see the person (those at the home itself, else those across the organisation) are told, else the fallback, and with no one at all the gap is audited (`care.refused_entry.unrouted`; ADR-0071 review rounds 2 and 3, C2-M1); only active staff are told; overdue, it escalates only to those who can act there, else to the longest-serving Registered Manager anywhere; a copy about nobody joins the review at its author's most recent location; the task lists each copy by kind, ids, refusal code, time, device and author, never its content; each author's copies count against 200 a day, a hand-over's against the owner; a copy the server cannot keep is counted in the device's report and said on its needs-attention list. Nothing on the record changes because of a copy, and no screen shows a copy yet (it comes with the task views of the web record, slice 9), so the device keeps every refused entry until it is dealt with there (H-134). Confirm the role, the priority, the fallback and the daily limit, and decide what the reviewer does with a copy (record the care again, correct the cause of the refusal, or close with a note) | CSO | [Hazard log](../clinical-safety/hazard-log.md) HZ-006 and HZ-008; `apps/api/src/services/refused-entries.ts` | Care mode on a live tenant | provisional: owner-accepted recommendation, pending CSO review at the testing phase (ADR-0132) |
| H-158 | Accept the hand-over of held entries ([care.md](../specs/care.md) section 6, Device integrity part 6; HZ-004, HZ-040; #25 review round 2, H1 and H2). Defaults in force: a holder of `care.held_entry.hand_over` (owner, Registered Manager, deputy, clinical lead or nurse; step-up and MFA), or the owner on a revoked device, signs in on the device holding another member's entries; the device sends its report from that sign-in first, binding the sender to the device. Each entry is taken only while the device's reports show an open hold for the owner, within the count of the latest report in that hold not sent by the sender (the owner's own preferred): the sender's own reports never set the count, so a hand-over with no other report is refused (#25 review round 3, item 1). Its time (when it was written on the device, else its event time) is taken within `sync.clock_drift_flag_minutes`: at or after the owner first used the device, at or before the report that set the count (not the hold's `waitingSince`, which is when the device held the entries, after they were written) and, on a revoked device, at or before the revocation; it is about someone in the owner's care within the offline window before the entry's own time (round 3, M1); it carries a hand-over id naming that owner, device and sender only, and is admitted in the entry's own transaction, so an entry refused after its admission uses none of the count; a copy of a refused entry sent with a hand-over is admitted the same way. It is written as the owner's (`created_by`) with the sender kept and shown as "Sent by" (`handed_over_by`), recorded in `handed_over_entry` and audited (`sync.entry.handed_over`); a round it names completes only on the owner's permission when the care was recorded. A refusal or a 404 keeps that entry held with the server's reason and the device sends the rest; any 403 stops it; nothing is parked as refused; an entry refused for what it is moves to needs attention with its copy sent naming the owner; the device first holds anything left in its upload queue and deletes its database only when no held, needs-attention or queued entry remains, each counted (round 3, M1 and M4). No screen shows "Sent by" yet: `handedOverBy` is returned with every entry and shown when the record views come (Phase 1 slice 9). Decide which roles may hand over, whether an owner who has left may still be named as the author, and whether a second holder's confirmation (another member signed in on the device vouching for the count) should let a hand-over through when only the sender has reported the hold, which is now refused: a decision for the CSO and DPO. (b) the evidence model (review M5): whether one PDF may stand as the written record of the conversation (20(3)(e)), the written notification's copy (20(6)) and correspondence at once, how to record who was told (the relevant person, or a representative only in 20(7)'s cases) and the reasonable support given (20(2)(b)), and whether one attempt is enough to finish as no contact; (c) one candour record per person: until it is built, an incident that harmed more than one person cannot close (review H1, fail safe); (d) whether a candour decision found after an incident has closed needs a route; (e) whether separation of duties applies to candour and investigation steps (review L6: today any manager, the person who raised it included, may take them); (f) whether dated investigation actions may go only to staff who manage incidents (review L8: today an owner who cannot complete the task is refused); review round 2 questions: (g) that one threshold decision covers only the person it names, never everyone affected (today a "not notifiable" record for one person leaves the others still to be told and the incident open); (h) that the relevant person must be one of the people recorded as affected, a representative being told in their place under 20(7) on the same record; (i) editing a finished record (#120 review round 3, H1): today a finished record's decision and relevant person cannot change; a record finished as not notifiable or no contact can be reopened (back to the threshold decision, or to telling the person) only with a reason of at least 20 characters, audited, which makes a candour notification decided not required a draft again and blocks closing; a closed record cannot be reopened, nor anything changed on a closed incident; its other fields (outcome, correspondence) can still be added to. Confirm these, the 20-character figure, and what happens to the documents a reopened record cites; (j) who owns an incident the duty of candour holds open (round 3, M2): today an urgent task after `candour.close_blocked_days` (Thapsus Care default 14 days from the incident, to verify), told on its first day to the Registered Managers who manage incidents at the location (with none, the owners), then every day to the Registered Managers and owners (founder, director or Nominated Individual) who manage incidents, until nothing holds it; never to clinical leads or team leaders. | H-68 | Accept two interim data-protection positions for Care mode, and hold go-live on a third. (1) Every validated-tool result for people in scope reaches the device (summary columns only, no answers) because a stream cannot pick the latest result per tool. (2) A device whose session ends with entries unsent keeps those entries only (everything else synced is cleared), encrypted (SQLCipher plus device authentication; for passcode-only devices see H-125), until their owner sends them or the owner or a manager deletes them, because a 401 cannot tell an expired session from a withdrawn one; a manager's revocation of the device is now a separate signal (H-156) (HZ-006, HZ-040). Nothing is deleted automatically: at `mobile.held_entries_max_days` (H-124) the hold escalates to a manager. Only the member of staff who wrote them can sign in and send them; deleting them needs the owner's or a manager's sign-in on the device, device authentication and a coded reason, and is recorded at the API as audit events, one per stage, with the coded reason only (`POST /v1/sync/discards`; the device keeps only a coded tombstone until the server has every stage; words given when removing one refused entry stay in the device's encrypted database); the device reports its held and unsent counts and when the hold started (`POST /v1/sync/device-report`), and while anything waits the server keeps a task due when the hold escalates, through the existing task engine (no new table), for a Registered Manager at the locations where the hold's owner or the reporter works or last worked, or for each Registered Manager when no location is known (H-142), the report naming the owner and those locations by opaque id; a hold whose session ended with a 401, `not_a_member` or `mfa_enrolment_required` is reported only when someone signs in on that device again (the owner, or another member of the organisation), so until then the server knows only the device's last report (H-141). A deletion is recorded as "failed" only while the entries can still be read on the device, and those entries are then never uploaded; the block on uploading them is a keystore write made before anything is deleted, and if it cannot be made nothing is deleted (H-143). A device with nothing unsent is still wiped. **Go-live blockers**, both now built ([care.md](../specs/care.md) section 6, Device integrity) and waiting on decisions: (a) the API revocation signal distinct from expiry (403 `device_revoked`): a revoked device with nothing unsent wipes, and one with entries unsent keeps those entries under the hold, never syncing again, until a hand-over or a recorded deletion, rather than deleting them at once (H-156); (b) the manager hand-over upload, which sends another member of staff's held entries to the server under the owner's name with the manager recorded, instead of the entries waiting on the device or being deleted (H-158). The earlier position on the offline window is closed: the API now mints the window into the signed sync token from the rate table and the streams read nothing from the device (security review S1) | DPO | [care.md](../specs/care.md) section 6; [records of processing](../compliance/records-of-processing.md) A2; `apps/mobile/src/app-state/held-work.ts` | Care mode on a live tenant; H-156 and H-158 must be accepted (the revocation signal and the manager hand-over upload are built) | open |

| H-150 | Confirm the window around each care plan round. Default in force: a round may be done from 30 minutes before its time (`care_plan.task_window_before_minutes`) until 60 minutes after (`care_plan.task_window_after_minutes`), then it is overdue and escalates once after `task.overdue_escalation_minutes` (30). Each window is worked out from the real instants of the round and its neighbours, never the clock face: where rounds come closer together than the window is long (hourly checks, or two times a clock change brings together), each side is cut to under half the real gap, so no instant is inside two windows and no window is ever nothing; consecutive times under 10 minutes apart on the clock face are refused when the plan is written, for every frequency (a weekly Monday 23:55 and Tuesday 00:00 included), and on the night the clocks go forward any two times that land on the same instant, or under 10 minutes apart, are one round (01:00 and 02:00, 01:30 and 02:30, 01:05 and 02:00). The alternative, deleting them at once with the rest, would lose care given but not recorded at the server. Also confirm who may revoke (owner, Registered Manager and deputy, with step-up; a location role only a device every member bound to it is its staff) and the reason codes (`lost_or_stolen`, `staff_left`, `access_withdrawn`, `replaced`, `other`). | H-303 | Decide what the phone must say when the outgoing handover is missing or not final, and whether HZ-024 needs a control on the phone (HZ-018, HZ-024). The phone receives a handover only once someone (or the worker, after the shift ends) has compiled it, and `locked_at` is empty until its shift ends; it cannot yet say "From an earlier plan" on a round task, as the web round does. the lead times start now. "Slice" numbers are the plan's; ADR-0080 to ADR-0097 are written by the
slice named in the plan. Phase 2 slices that need further actions take the lane blocks the plan
reserves (H-550 onwards).

| H-550 | Decide the second-check policy for MAR entries: whether an entry "Not yet checked" may be given before its check (default: yes, with the notice beside the dose; a tenant may choose to hold such entries), how soon the check is due and who is told when it is not done, whether "Check found a difference" should stop the dose (default: yes, until a corrected version replaces it), whether a stop needs a second check too (default: no; it takes effect with its source and reason), and whether a future stop or replacement should itself be checked before it takes effect (default: the current version stays checkable, correctable and givable until then; #135 review); whether a stop still to come should carry over to a correction made before it (default: yes, always, so a change never cancels a stop; a course extended past its stop is a new entry from the stop); that only one version is ever in force, and that a change or stop while a later version is scheduled is refused unless it withdraws that version and says why (default, more restrictive; #135 third review); that no dose is given from a variable-dose entry until its dosing record exists (default: blocked), and the interim instruction its notice gives ("Ask the prescriber for today's dose and record it as a new MAR entry, stopping this one first"); **the release gate**: slice 2 is not released to a tenant without slice 3's dosing record, so variable-dose medicines (warfarin) are never left with only that interim instruction (confirm the gate, or that the interim instruction is acceptable); that a correction after a check found a difference takes effect at once, for the rest of the day, with a notice that day to check the day's doses with the nurse in charge (default; #135 fourth review: a correction fixes a transcription error, so the evening dose is not dropped), and whether the corrected entry's earlier dose that day needs more than a notice; and the dosing record for variable doses (each new dosing instruction, for example warfarin after an INR test, second-checked before any dose is given from it; slice 3) | CSO | [medicines.md](../specs/medicines.md) rule 6; `UncheckedEntryPolicy` in `packages/domain/src/medicines/mar-entry.ts`; HZ-005 (slice 2) | The API's second check (slice 16) and the round (slices 48, 53) | provisional: owner-accepted recommendation, pending CSO review at the testing phase (ADR-0132) |

| H-552 | Review the reconciliation rules: every MAR entry and every source line decided by a person; the source kinds (a verbal instruction is not a source; what the person or family says is not enough on its own; the previous setting's MAR is allowed); the discharge letter or a reason on a return from hospital; which sources show what is prescribed now (on a return, only the discharge letter when there is one; otherwise a prescription, the pharmacy's MAR, a letter, the GP record or the prescriber's written instruction), and only when received on or after the event or marked current with a reason; recording on each source line what it says (start, continue, change, stop or unclear) and refusing any decision a line does not say, whatever the note; starts only from a current prescriber's or pharmacy line, with no confirmation route (default, more restrictive than a continue); and who may confirm a continue that only labels, the previous MAR or the family support, or that no source lists (default: a note and the prescriber or a clinician named, with how and when); a stop only from a line that says so, never absence; one entry per dm+d product, so a start of a product already on the MAR is refused with no note to override it, and a change keeps the product (a switch, such as warfarin to apixaban, is a stop on the old product's line and a start on the new one's) (#135 third review); a continue or a change refused while it keeps a change or stop scheduled before the reconciliation that has not been checked against the sources and said to stand, and a stop or change giving the reason it withdraws what was scheduled (#135 fourth and fifth reviews); a note to continue a medicine a source shows as another product or brand; queries block completion; the completer confirms the allergies were compared; the person's involvement; "withheld until review" handled as stop and start again; who may complete one (medicines competence); and the reading of NICE SC1 sections 1.7 and 1.14 as the master prompt cites them (the build could not reach the NICE pages) | CSO with a pharmacist | [medicines.md](../specs/medicines.md) rules 7 to 12; `reconciliation.ts`; HZ-121 (slice 2) | Reconciliation in a real tenant (slices 16, 52) | provisional: owner-accepted recommendation, pending CSO review at the testing phase (ADR-0132) |
| H-553 | Confirm the MAR schedule model: every day, every n days, weekly on named days every n weeks, monthly on a day (the last day of a shorter month, flagged); times written out rather than "every n hours"; a dose per prescribed time; section 18's schedule type held as regular or when required, with a variable dose as a dose kind and covert, self-administration and homely remedies as records of their own (slice 38); when the clocks going forward bring two prescribed times together, both doses kept and flagged (with H-22); and the day count of every-n-days and fortnightly medicines kept from the last dose actually due, across changes, through every day or when required and across a stop and restart, with a weekday or day-of-month move (even daily to weekly) counting as a new count that needs a recorded reason (default, more restrictive); and the refusals that replace handling the complicated sequences (#135 third review; each default more restrictive): no change part-way through a day on which either version has a regular dose (it takes effect from 00:00 the next day, so a change wanted the same evening, or regular to when required that day, waits a day: is that acceptable, and what do staff do meanwhile?); no restart part-way through a day after a dose was due under the entry that stopped; no change when no version is in force; and one entry per product, so a regular and a when-required entry of the same VMP (for example lorazepam at night and when required) cannot both be on the MAR (is a combined entry, or an exception, needed?). From the #135 fourth review: a change keeps the course end, its own or that of a version it withdraws (no change drops or extends it, even when the new prescription gives a later end; the course continues as a new entry from its end); and a same-day change, such as after a return from hospital at 14:00, still takes effect from 00:00 the next day, the old version telling staff "A change starts tomorrow: check today's remaining doses with the nurse in charge" (the stricter of the two options, rather than letting a reconciliation's change take effect that day with an answer about the day's doses): is a notice enough, or should the old version's remaining doses that day be held? From the follow-up and fifth review: no dose of a new entry is due before it was recorded (an entry recorded at 14:00 from 00:00 has no 08:00 dose that day, shown as "Before this entry was recorded: not due"), unless the transcription gives the reason that day's earlier doses may still be needed, when they stay due with a notice to check with the nurse in charge (the same-day route): is that the right control on the day of admission, and who may give the reason? A course the discharge letter extends is recorded as the existing entry continued to its end and a new entry started from then on the letter's line (default, rather than a change allowed to extend the end when a current line says so): is that acceptable when the letter has a single line for the medicine? For the CSO to confirm (#135 sixth review): who may give the same-day reason (default: the transcriber, whose entry a second person then checks); the single-line extension above; the refusal of a reconciliation begun before midnight and completed after a change took effect at 00:00 (default: refused, naming the version, until those decisions are redone); and the dose before midnight on an admission recorded just after it (a 23:30 dose when the entry is recorded at 00:30), which the same-day route cannot keep due: is a missed dose there acceptable, or is a route needed for the evening before? | CSO with a pharmacist | [medicines.md](../specs/medicines.md) sections 2 and 4.1 rules 3 to 5; `schedule.ts` (slice 2) | The medication tables (slice 16) | provisional: owner-accepted recommendation, pending CSO review at the testing phase (ADR-0132) |

Built (batch 3b, the safer reading after its clinical review): a new or changed when-required entry needs its 24-hour maximum from the prescription or PRN protocol (`prn_limit_required`), in the domain, the API, the web form and the import; an interval alone is refused, because an interval can be overridden with a reason and caps no daily total, so a paracetamol-type entry given every four hours round the clock would pass. | H-1067 | Confirm that a dose that has already happened is recorded and flagged rather than refused: every correction, and a dose staff record after giving it (`givenBeforeRecorded`), meet every administration rule, but the rules that refuse a dose about to be given (its window, an earlier dose outstanding, the entry in force, the dosing record, the maximum, and the competence of whoever gave it) flag it instead, so a false record never stands and the next dose's intervals and maximum see it (default built, #162 second clinical review). Every such record is escalated to the nurse in charge: `warning` to review, `urgent` over the maximum or with no checked dose to compare it with; confirm those severities and who receives each (with H-554 and slice 48). So that the route for a dose given before it was recorded cannot stand in for the round (#162 third clinical review), and a correction to a dose outcome cannot get round it (the re-check: every such correction follows exactly the same rules), the default built asks for a reason of 20 characters or more, a time given strictly before the server's time of recording, a recorder with current competence (refused otherwise), the named giver's competence at the time given (flagged), and flags every use `given_before_recorded`, counted per member beside competence overrides for the override report and escalated urgently when it broke any rule. Confirm the reason length, and whether the report should also alert when one member's uses pass a threshold the tenant sets. Every correction is now a reviewable event too (the re-check's second round): a 20-character reason for any correction, flagged `corrected` with what it changes and counted per member; one that removes, lowers or moves a dose recorded as taken is escalated to the nurse in charge, `urgent` when the entry has a maximum, an interval or the paracetamol tag (it frees room for another dose) and otherwise a `warning`, so a routine amount correction on a regular medicine raises a warning: accept that, or refuse it (for example counting it for the report only); breaches of the maximum, intervals or scheduled gap that a correction creates or hides are flagged and escalated urgently; and a correction of only the giver is flagged and counted, with a warning, not re-checking the competence of the member first named. Also confirm the record delay: on the round a dose may be recorded up to `medicines.record_delay_tolerance_minutes` (30 minutes, marked to verify; carers routinely record 10 to 20 minutes after a round) after it was given as an ordinary record, one dated further back is refused and goes by the flagged route, and one dated more than `sync.clock_drift_flag_minutes` after now is refused; an ordinary round record must pass every rule at both the time given and the time recorded, or goes by the flagged route, and lateness is judged at the later of the two, so a dose recorded late shows as late even when it was given in time; and the delay and the route apply to every outcome, so a refusal, or a dose the person took themselves, recorded more than 30 minutes later goes by the flagged route with a reason and a warning to the nurse in charge (in supported living, where doses taken by the person are often recorded at a later visit, that may raise many warnings: say whether those outcomes need a longer delay). A record by the flagged route is also checked at the time it is recorded, and where the rules give a different answer it is escalated urgently: a scheduled dose recorded after its window, or a when-required dose with another dose given between the two times, will usually differ, so most records by the route for such doses escalate urgently; confirm that, or say which differences should only warn. The CSO confirms the 30 minutes. | H-1245 | Allow `isd.digital.nhs.uk` through the egress rules for the dm+d job only. TRUD is a download source that receives no personal data, so it is not a sub-processor (Phase 2 plan, data protection table, slice 10). As built (H-1241): the VPC has no egress firewall and a rule by host name needs Cloud NGFW, which the network does not have, so nothing in Google Cloud narrows the job's egress. The job reaches TRUD over HTTPS from the NAT addresses, and the application refuses any host but `TRUD_ALLOWED_HOSTS` (`isd.digital.nhs.uk`), redirects included. Recommended answer: the application's host allow-list is enough for a download source that receives no personal data. If a network rule is ever wanted, Cloud NGFW can hold one for this job alone: the Standard tier's FQDN objects in a network firewall policy, billed on the traffic it handles, or the Enterprise tier, billed per endpoint by the hour and far dearer; check Google's current prices before choosing | Brian | [integrations.md](../specs/integrations.md) section 11, "Release download" rule 3 | The job reaching TRUD | provisional: owner-accepted recommendation, pending review at the testing phase |

## Per tenant at onboarding

Sign the DPA and the DPIA (with the AI addendum if AI is enabled), complete the DCB0160 deployment
assessment ([template](../clinical-safety/dcb0160-deployment-template.md)), enrol shared devices in
MDM, sign off imported data, confirm photos for every person, approve role design and run the
[tenant onboarding](../runbooks/tenant-onboarding.md) checklist.

Sign the DPA and the DPIA (with the AI addendum if AI is enabled), complete the DCB0160 deployment
assessment ([template](../clinical-safety/dcb0160-deployment-template.md)), enrol shared devices in
MDM, sign off imported data, confirm photos for every person, approve role design and run the
[tenant onboarding](../runbooks/tenant-onboarding.md) checklist.

Recommended answers, in force until then: (1) one person confirms a match, with a fresh step-up, never to their own staff file, and only someone who manages the staff file when Thapsus-HR gave a leaving date; no second person, because a match is rejected or unlinked as easily and a second person would delay leavers; (2) a leaving date that is not a real day counts as today; (3) a start date that moves across today is held for review, never put back; (4) rule 13's incoming-shift checks are not needed while the rota stays with Thapsus Care; (5) an absence from Thapsus-HR with no end date blocks until Thapsus-HR ends it, and an unknown type blocks as other and is raised; (6) an absence no longer listed stops blocking automatically, but not in a run with unreadable records and never more than 5 current ones at once: those are held and one person with `integrations.hr_link.manage` releases them with step-up, a coded reason and the ids they reviewed, after Thapsus-HR's list is read again (one already over goes at once); (7) a wrong leaving date is withdrawn by one person who manages the staff file, with step-up and a reason, only when Thapsus-HR positively shows no leaving; (8) after a disconnect, copies of Thapsus-HR's absences stay and the rota says its HR data is out of date until one person with `integrations.hr_link.manage` ends them the same way (no second person: the release is audited with its count and the copies' history stays) | Clinical safety officer | HZ-133 in the hazard log (slice 86) | A linked tenant going live | owner-accepted recommendation, pending CSO review at the testing phase (06/11/2026) |
| H-1441 | Rate HZ-140 and review its controls before a linked tenant pays through Thapsus-HR. Recommended answers, in force until then: (1) one person with `integrations.hr_link.manage` moves the route to payroll, with a fresh step-up and only for periods with no pay run yet, with no second person, because each run's route is then fixed and every run is still checked and locked by a second person before anything is sent; (2) a member with no Thapsus-HR rate blocks the run (the "no role rate" exception, which cannot be overridden for such a period) rather than falling back to the organisation's rule set, and the check uses the lowest rate in force during the period; (3) a lock is refused while any unit kind has no pay code or any member with units is unmatched, and a member unmatched after the lock holds the period and alerts pay approvers; (4) a correction takes its original's route and replaces the whole period's items in Thapsus-HR; (5) pay codes are mapped by a pay approver, with step-up; (6) the severity is Significant rather than Major, because pay that cannot reach payroll is never locked and pay approvers are told whenever it waits | Clinical safety officer | HZ-140 in the hazard log; [ADR-0131](../decisions/0131-pay-through-thapsus-hr.md) | A linked tenant paying through Thapsus-HR | owner-accepted recommendation, pending CSO review at the testing phase (06/11/2026) |

| H-1311 | Confirm the duress gesture: a long press on Check in, held 1.5 seconds (`DURESS_HOLD_MS`), giving the same haptic, words and screen as a check-in; screen-reader users use the platform's own long-press gesture. The accessibility review of slice 71 notes that TalkBack may announce a long-press action on Check in only, that a hardware keyboard or switch access has no long press, and that a slow press or tremor could send duress by accident. Acting on release was tried and dropped in review: a cancelled slow press would still send duress, and a screen reader's long press could leave the next tap sending it; so it acts when the hold registers, and the answer comes while the finger is still down, which someone watching closely could notice. Alternatives: a private per-person setting agreed with the manager (long press, or a pre-agreed check-in code a coercer cannot tell apart) and the hold time. Recommended: accept the long press for the pilot, offer the per-person setting before general release, train staff on it (H-1039), test it with lone workers and screen-reader users, and record the decision in the accessibility checklist | CSO with Brian | `LoneWorkingPanel.tsx`; H-1034 | As H-1310 | Open, recommended answer in force |

| H-1430 | Build narrowing for a member whose phone would pass PowerSync's buckets per connection (ADR-0134): today the token is refused by name (`sync_scope_too_large`) and the phone shows a failed sync; instead narrow the whole-home reach to the homes they work at (then apply the whole-home cover, so no assigned person drops off) and show it on the phone: a notice in the sync banner, in words, with an icon, saying that other homes are not on the phone; the banner says why since the slice 68 rebuild ("This phone cannot sync"); its words are English constants in `@harbour/ui-native` like the rest of the banner, not yet through ICU (Welsh), and a Maestro journey for the state is still to add. With medicines on the phone, a care worker assigned to more than about 30 people is refused, so the fallback that names no one in `entries_write_person_ids` above 100 (`MAX_WRITE_PERSON_IDS`) is out of a phone's reach until narrowing exists | Brian (engineering) | ADR-0134; HZ-002 | None | open |
| H-1431 | Confirm that a member whose access passes a phone's bucket limit (with medicines on the phone, about 33 or more assigned people at the 31-day offline window, about 50 at 14 days, about 60 at 7 days; varying with the calendar date and able to start at UTC midnight, mid-shift) is refused offline sync by name and has no offline MAR or care record until H-1430's narrowing exists; that the phone tells them to check the paper MAR or ask the nurse in charge before giving medicines; that the refusal is audited (`sync.token.refused`) for the Registered Manager to see; and that a warning before the limit (on the phone and to the manager) and notifying the manager on refusal are still to build (HZ-138, HZ-003, HZ-004). Recommended: accept for the pilot | CSO | ADR-0134; HZ-138 | None | Owner-accepted recommendation, pending CSO review at the testing phase |
| H-1526 | Translate the phone's training strings into Welsh (keys `mobile.training.*`, marked untranslated in `mobile.cy.status.json`) before a Welsh tenant uses them (slice 72 leftover; H-37) | Welsh translator, Brian | `packages/i18n/src/messages/mobile.cy.json` | Welsh tenants going live | Open |
| H-1527 | Decide the phone's tab bar once medicines land (batch 5): a member who holds everything sees Today, Round, Medicines, Handover, Shifts, Training, People and Settings, eight tabs, more than a phone's tab bar holds comfortably (platform guidance is three to five). Recommended: keep Today, Round, Medicines and People as tabs and move Handover, Shifts, Training and Settings under a "More" tab, chosen by role | Brian with the design-partner Registered Manager | `apps/mobile/src/app/(care)/_layout.tsx` | None | Open |
